PROVIDED THAT if SCDC fails to inspect the Sports Pitches, Play Areas and Formal Park (as applicable) within 40 Working Days of receipt of the notice of invitation from the Owners or fails to issue a Council Completion Certificate within 40 Working Days of the inspection where no remedial works have been identified then the Council Completion Certificate shall be deemed to have been issued at the end of those
specified periods **PROVIDED FURTHER THAT** the inspection procedure identified in paragraphs 4.1 and 4.2 shall be repeated until such time as SCDC issue a Council Completion Certificate or a Council Completion Certificate shall been deemed to have been issued in relation to the Sports Pitches, Play Areas and Formal Park (as applicable).

5.	Following issue or deemed issue of a Council Completion Certificate in respect of the Sports Pitches, Play Areas and Formal Park (as applicable), the Owners shall:

5.1	maintain the Sports Pitches, Play Areas and Formal Park (as applicable) for the Maintenance Period to the reasonable satisfaction of SCDC; and

5.2	allow access to the Sports Pitches, Play Areas and Formal Park (as applicable) (free of charge but on a permissive basis only) to members of the public each day for the purpose of recreation and play (unless SCDC agrees otherwise in writing).

6.	On expiration of the Maintenance Period the Owners shall:

6.1	serve notice on SCDC inviting them to inspect the Sports Pitches, Play Areas and Formal Park (as applicable) and issue a Council Final Certificate confirming that such works have been maintained to their reasonable satisfaction;

6.2	if SCDC chooses to inspect the Sports Pitches, Play Areas and Formal Park (as applicable) and identifies necessary remedial works, to complete such remedial works to the reasonable satisfaction of SCDC as soon as reasonably practicable;

6.3	upon completion of any remedial works, to serve notice on SCDC inviting them to inspect the remedial works identified by them pursuant to paragraph 6.2 of this Part 7 and issue a Council Final Certificate confirming that the Sports Pitches, Play Areas and Formal Park (as applicable) have been maintained to the reasonable satisfaction of SCDC

**PROVIDED THAT** if SCDC fails to inspect the Sports Pitches, Play Areas and Formal Park (as applicable) within 40 Working Days of invitation from the Owners or fails to issue a Council Final Certificate within 40 Working Days of the inspection where no remedial works have been identified then the Council Final Certificate shall be deemed to have been issued at the end of those specified periods **PROVIDED FURTHER THAT** the inspection procedure identified in paragraphs 6.1 and 6.2 shall be repeated until such time as SCDC issue a Council Final Certificate or a Council Final Certificate shall been deemed to have been issued in relation to the Sports Pitches, Play Areas and Formal Park (as applicable).

7.	The Owners shall maintain the Sports Pitches, Play Areas and Formal Park (as applicable) in accordance with the relevant specification unless and until such time that the relevant Open Space Land Transfer is completed to SCDC an Alternative Body or Management Company who shall thereafter become responsible for the maintenance of the Sports Pitches, Play Areas and Formal Park (as applicable) in accordance with the relevant specification **PROVIDED THAT** transfer of the relevant Open Space Land may be completed on a phased basis following the issue of Council Final Certificates in relation to such part of the Open Space Land.

8.	On expiration of the Maintenance Period in respect of the Sports Pitches and the All Weather Pitch before any Certificate of Final Completion is issued the Owners will procure an independent surveyor’s report addressed to SCDC or an Alternative Body or Management Company from a reputable consultant (and which Consultant will enter into a collateral warranty in favour of SCDC (or where applicable the Alternative Body or Management Company).

8.1	Where specialist design is involved and/or where specialist contractors are engaged the Owners shall use Reasonable Endeavours to procure that with effect from the
Certificate of Practical Completion for the Sports Pitches or if later from the date of
transfer the benefit of all guarantees and warranties relating to the design and
construction/laying out of the Sports Pitches are assigned or otherwise, passed on to
a applicable

8.1.1 SCDC; or
8.1.2 the Alternative Body, or
8.1.3 the Management Company

PROVIDED THAT in the event of an Insolvency Event on the part of the designers or
contractors of the Sports pitches such that the Owners having used Reasonable
Endeavours have not obtained assignment or otherwise of all guarantees and
warranties relating to the design and/or construction of the Sports Pitches the Owners
shall obtain the necessary insurance for the benefit of SCDC the Alternative Body or
the Management Company (as applicable and if requested in writing by such entities)
in relation to defective construction and/or latent defective design as may be available
from commercial insurers at reasonably commercial rates and terms for a insured term
of not more than the period that the warranty or guarantee would otherwise endure for
and on such insurance being put in place the obligation to obtain guarantees and
warranties will cease to apply.

9. The Owners will use Reasonable Endeavours to obtain a collateral warranty for each
of the NEAPS/LEAPS where these are available at normal and standard commercial
terms and such that on expiration of the Maintenance Period in respect of the NEAPS
/LEAPS and before any relevant Certificate of Final Completion is issued then
collateral warranties are given in favour of SCDC or Alternative Body or Management
Company where these are available at normal and standard commercial terms.

PART 8 - COVENANTS RELATING TO DELIVERY REQUIREMENTS

1. The Owners covenant with SCDC;

1.1 Prior to Occupation of 350 Dwellings to complete the Sports Pitches which shall be
evidence by the issuing or the deemed issue of the Council Completion Certificate;
and

1.2 Within 40 Working Days of receipt of a written demand from SCDC that evidences that
SCDC has not secured alternative funding for the Cricket Purpose and/or that monies
to fund works to secure completion of the Cricket Purpose are still required the
Owners shall pay to SCDC the Cricket Upgrade Contribution towards the Cricket
Purpose only PROVIDED THAT the written demand from SCDC shall only be validly
served and operate to require payment by the Owners under this paragraph where it is
served:

1.2.1 after the date of the transfer of the Sports Pitches to SCDC the Town
Council an Alternative Body or the Management Company (as applicable);
and

1.2.2 and the Council has not already served a written demand pursuant to
paragraph 1.3 of this Part 8 of Schedule 14

AND for the avoidance of doubt where the Remaining Sports Balance is nil then the
Owners shall be released from the obligation in this paragraph 1.2 of this Part 8 of this
Schedule 14.

1.3 In the event that SCDC does not serve a written demand requiring payment of the
Cricket Upgrade Contribution pursuant to paragraph 1.2 of this Part 8 of Schedule 14
above or the Remaining Sports Balance exceeds the maximum amount of the Cricket
Upgrade Contribution then SCDC may serve a written demand on the Owners requesting payment of the Remaining Sports Balance (if any) to SCDC to be used for the Northstowe Sports Purpose and the Owners shall pay to SCDC the Remaining Sports Balance or the balance remaining after payment of the Cricket Upgrade Contribution (as applicable) within 40 Working Days of the date of receipt of the written demand served under this paragraph 1.3 of this Part 8 of this Schedule 14 to be used towards the Northstowe Sports Purpose only PROVIDED THAT the written demand from SCDC shall only be validly served and operate to require payment by the Owners under this paragraph where it is served:

1.3.1 after the date of the transfer of the Sports Pitches to SCDC the Town Council an Alternative Body or the Management Company (as applicable); AND

1.3.2 the Council has not already served a written demand pursuant to paragraph 1.2 of this Part 8 of this Schedule 14

AND for the avoidance of doubt where the Remaining Sports Balance is nil then the Owners shall be released from the obligation in this paragraph 1.3 of this Part 8 of this Schedule 14.

1.4 Prior to Occupation of 350 Dwellings to complete the Formal Park and NEAP which shall be evidenced by the issuing or the deemed issue of the Council Completion Certificate

1.5 No later than whichever of the following shall be the first to occur to complete each respective LEAP and/or SIP which shall be evidenced by the issuing or the deemed issue of the Council Completion Certificate in respect of each respective LEAP and/or SIP:

1.5.1 Occupation of 50% of the Dwellings within the Development Parcel that the LEAP and/or SIP forms part of; or

1.5.2 Occupation of 75% of the Dwellings which are adjacent to or have a frontage facing the respective LEAP and/or SIP.

1.6 No later than whichever of the following shall be the first to occur to complete the each respective LAP which shall be evidenced by the issuing or the deemed issue of the Council Completion Certificate in respective of each respective LAP:

1.6.1 Occupation of 50% of the Dwellings within the Development Parcel that the LAP forms part of; or

1.6.2 Occupation of 75% of the Dwellings which are adjacent to or have a frontage facing the respective LAP.

**PART 9 - INCIDENTAL OPEN SPACE**

1. The Owners covenant with SCDC as follows:

1.1 The areas of Incidental Open Space shall either be maintained as follows:

1.1.1 The Owners shall retain the freehold interest in the Incidental Open Space and appoint a Management Company to manage and maintain on behalf of the Owners; or

1.1.2 the leasehold interest of not more than 10 years in the Incidental Open Space will be transferred to the Management Company; or
1.1.3 the Incidental Open Space will be transferred as part of the land comprising a Dwelling where a Dwelling is located adjacent to any area of Incidental Open Space and the owners of the Dwelling shall be responsible for the maintenance of such area of Incidental Open Space.

PROVIDED THAT nothing shall prevent the Owners from choosing a variety of transferees referred to in this paragraph 1.1 of this Part 9 of this Schedule 14 for different parts of the Incidental Open Space PROVIDED FURTHER THAT the Owners shall maintain the area(s) of Incidental Open Space until such time as the relevant area(s) are transferred pursuant to the provisions of this paragraph 1.1 of this Part 9 of this Schedule 14.

1.2 The relevant transfer referred to in paragraph 1.1 of this Part 9 of this Schedule 14 above shall take place as follows:

1.2.1 In relation to transfers to the Management Company no later than Occupation of 85% of the Dwellings provided that nothing shall prevent the Owners from transferring the Incidental Open Space to the Management Company in tranches;

1.2.2 In relation to transfers to plot purchasers of Dwellings located adjacent to any area of Incidental Open Space the area of Incidental Open Space shall form part of the property which is the subject of the transfer to the plot.

PART 10 - TERMS OF REFERENCE FOR THE STEERING GROUP FOR THE MANAGEMENT AND MAINTENANCE OF THE PUBLIC REALM

1. Purpose

SCDC and Gallagher have agreed to establish, as soon as reasonably practicable after the approval of the Northstowe Phase 1 Management and Maintenance Strategy, a Steering Group of relevant qualified persons to deliver the actions necessary to deliver the Northstowe Phase 1 Management and Maintenance Strategy.

The purpose of the Steering Group is to oversee the Northstowe Phase 1 Management and Maintenance Purposes and to ensure that the SCDC, County Council, Northstowe Parish/ Town Council (where such body is established), Gallagher and any other relevant organisation act collaboratively on the decisions that have to be made in managing and maintaining the Public Realm there for the benefit of the residents of the Development.

2. Representation

2.1 Initially, the Steering Group shall consist of one representative from each of the following:

2.1.1 The County Council
2.1.2 SCDC
2.1.3 Gallagher
2.1.4 The Town Council where such a body has been established at the time the Steering Group is established
2.1.5 Any other Alternative Body with a legal interest in the Public Realm

2.2 New Steering Group members may be added as the need arises by mutual agreement between the parties on the Steering Group. The Steering Group will meet every month unless decided otherwise by the parties on the Steering Group.
2.3 Approvals by the Steering Group shall unless stated otherwise be decisions approved/agreed by a majority of the parties on the Steering Group SAVE THAT in the event that Gallagher acting reasonably is not one of the parties in that majority then Gallagher may if it wishes the majority decision not to apply refer the matter to an Expert for determination and in such cases the approval shall be suspended and shall not be capable of being relied and acted upon unless and until the decision of the majority has been confirmed as reasonable by the Expert.

3 Terms of Reference

3.1 The initial terms of reference of Steering Group will be as follows but this will not prevent the Steering Group adding to or amending the terms of reference at a later date PROVIDED THAT any such additions or amendments are reasonable and necessary.

3.2 To ensure the Northstowe Phase 1 Management and Maintenance Strategy is implemented according to the agreed regimes and standards contained within it.

3.3 To explore all funding mechanisms in order to supplement the Northstowe Phase 1 Maintenance Contribution and in making recommendations (or otherwise acting within its terms of reference) the Steering Group shall always give priority to pursuing and utilising (or securing the pursuit and utilisation of) Alternative Public Realm Funding to undertake the Northstowe Phase 1 Management and Maintenance Purposes rather than using the Northstowe Phase 1 Maintenance Contribution.

3.4 To receive and review monthly update reports on management and maintenance issues provided by the contractors and organisations managing and maintaining the Public Realm

3.5 To review the operation of the Northstowe Phase 1 Management and Maintenance Strategy from time to time taking into account relevant performance indicators and targets

3.6 To liaise with any Parish Councils whose administrative boundaries abut the Site.

PART 11 - SPORTS PITCHES CONTRIBUTION

1 Where the Sports Pitches provided pursuant to this Schedule 14 include the Informal Single Court MUGA then the definition of the Sports Pitches Contribution for the purposes of this Schedule 14 shall decrease by £37,675 PROVIDED ALWAYS that the definition of the Community Building and Sports Pavilion Contributions Cap and Community Building Contribution and the Community Building Actual Contribution and the Community Building Costs Cap and the Community Building Maximum Spend for the purposes of Schedule 8 increases by a corresponding amount.
SCHEDULE 15 - TEMPORARY WASTE FACILITIES AND WASTE AND RECYCLING BINS

RELEVANT DEFINITIONS

"Temporary Waste Facilities Contribution" means the sum of £24,000 (twenty four thousand British pounds) to be used towards Temporary Waste Facilities Purposes only;

"Temporary Waste Facilities Purposes" means for the provision of temporary waste facilities accommodating shoes clothes or books, or other types of waste as may be agreed by the Owners to be located in a position(s) within the Site as shown indicatively by blue circles on Plan 5 attached to this Agreement or in the vicinity of the Site in order to serve the Development;

"Waste and Recycling Bins Contribution" means the sum of £140,250 (one hundred and forty thousand two hundred and fifty British pounds) to be used towards Waste and Recycling Bins Purposes only;

"Waste and Recycling Bins Purposes" means for the provision of waste and recycling bins for each Dwelling forming part of the Development.

1. TEMPORARY WASTE FACILITIES CONTRIBUTION

1.1 Prior to Occupation of 100 Dwellings, the Owners shall pay the Temporary Waste Facilities Contribution to SCDC or at SCDC's nomination the County Council to be used towards Temporary Waste Facilities only.

2. WASTE AND RECYCLING BINS CONTRIBUTION

2.1 Prior to Occupation of any Dwelling within a Development Parcel the Owners shall pay to SCDC or at SCDC's nomination the County Council £93.50 per Dwelling within that Development Parcel as evidenced by Reserved Matters Approvals for that Development Parcel to be used towards the Waste and Recycling Bins Purposes only PROVIDED THAT the total amount that is paid in relation to the whole Development does not exceed the Waste and Recycling Bins Contribution.

1.  WEBBS HOLE SLUICE WORKS

1.1 Prior to Commencement of any of the Dwellings the Owners shall serve written notice
    on SCDC making a formal offer to SCDC to:

    1.1.1 undertake and complete the Webbs Holes Sluice Works SUBJECT TO the
          following being accepted and agreed by SCDC in SCDC's written response
          to the offer made pursuant to this paragraph 1.1:

          (a) SCDC shall in their written response to the offer made by the
              Owners demonstrate that they have secured the necessary rights
              over any third party land and interests required for the carrying out
              of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works; and
(b) SCDC shall in their written response to the offer unconditionally and irrevocably guarantee to bear the Adjusted Works Costs and the Owners' Project Management Fee and shall in their written response propose an instalment repayment period schedule for the Adjusted Works Costs and Project Management Fee and the first instalment shall be made no later than Completion of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works to the reasonable satisfaction of SCDC; and

(c) SCDC shall in their written response provide the Owners with any technical work regarding the design of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works that either (i) exists and is held within the control of SCDC or (ii) which SCDC could reasonably obtain at the date that SCDC write their response to the offer made as referred to above.

2. The Owners and SCDC acknowledge and agree that subject to 1.1.1 (b) and subject to paragraph 4.5.3 of this Schedule 16 below the Owners overall liability in respect of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works shall not exceed the Webbs Hole Sluice Contribution Costs Cap.

3. In the event that SCDC declines or otherwise does not accept the offer for the Owners to carry out the Webbs Hole Sluice Works as provided for in paragraph 1.1 above WHICH FOR THE AVOIDANCE OF DOUBT shall include SCDC not being able to provide confirmation of the requirements listed at items 1.1.1(a) to 1.1.1(c) then SCDC covenants with the Owners to accept the Webbs Hole Sluice Contribution in lieu of the Owners carrying out the Webbs Hole Sluice Works and if the Webbs Hole Sluice Contribution is paid to SCDC by the Owners then the Owners and SCDC agree and acknowledge that the following provisions of paragraphs 3.1 to 3.4 shall apply:

3.1 SCDC shall use all Reasonable Endeavours to complete or procure completion such that the Webbs Hole Sluice Works are completed prior to Occupation of 350 Dwellings; and

3.2 any obligation on the Owners to carry out the Webbs Hole Sluice Works pursuant to this Schedule shall cease to have effect; and

3.3 the Owners shall pay the Webbs Hole Sluice Pump Contribution to SCDC within 20 Working Days of receipt of written evidence from SCDC that SCDC has let a unconditional contract for the Webbs Hole Sluice Works to be carried out and PROVIDED THAT the said written evidence shall also include written evidence of the anticipated Webbs Hole Sluice Works Costs; and

3.4 SCDC shall use the Webbs Hole Sluice Contribution towards the cost of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works and for no other purpose.

4. In the event that SCDC accepts the offer made pursuant to paragraph 1.1 and the SCDC written response to the offer addresses all of the points set out in paragraph 1.1.1(a) to 1.1.1 (c) inclusive then the Owners shall:

4.1 submit for approval the Webbs Hole Sluice Specification in writing to SCDC which shall be approved or be deemed to be approved pursuant to the Approval Process; and

4.2 carry out the Webbs Hole Sluice Works in accordance with the approved or deemed approved Webbs Hole Sluice Specification which may include any variation approved or deemed approved by SCDC pursuant to the Approval Process; and

4.3 in carrying out and completing the Webbs Hole Sluice Works incur and pay for any External Costs but shall be entitled to reclaim from SCDC such External Costs reasonably incurred as part of the Adjusted Works Costs;
4.4 the Owners shall notify the Surveyor of all site meetings relating to the construction and equipping of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works;
4.5 the Owners shall permit the Surveyor:
    4.5.1 to attend and to put forward his/her views at all site meetings during the course of construction and fitting out/equipping of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works;
    4.5.2 on reasonable notice to inspect the Webbs Hole Sluice Works during the course of construction/fitting out;
    4.5.3 to suggest items for inclusion in any necessary schedule of defects issued to the contractor in respect of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works and the Owners acknowledge that the costs of remedying any Defects shall be undertaken at the Owners' expense and shall not be taken into account in determining if the Webbs Hole Sluice Works Costs Cap has been reached or exceeded;
    4.5.4 to stipulate any items or areas where the Webbs Hole Sluice Works does not meet the agreed Webbs Hole Sluice Works Specification (which forms part of the contract for the construction of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works) and then SCDC may serve on the Owners a written notice which shall specify those parts of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works Specification that have not been met and the Owners shall within 40 Working Days from the date of service of such notice (or such other period as SCDC may agree) carry out such works as are necessary to secure compliance with the Webbs Hole Sluice Works Specification and the Owners shall fund any and all reasonable subsequent inspections of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works until the approved the Webbs Hole Sluice Works Specification shall have been met PROVIDED FURTHER THAT the costs of such inspections do not result in the Webbs Hole Sluice Contributions Cost Cap being exceeded.

4.6 Not less than 20 Working Days prior to the anticipated issue of a Certificate of Practical Completion in respect of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works the Owners shall in writing invite the Surveyor to inspect the Webbs Hole Sluice Works and identify any Defects requiring attention and FOR THE AVOIDANCE OF DOUBT subject to para 4.7 below the Owners shall be entitled to issue the Certificate of Practical Completion in the event that the Owners are the supervising body under the terms of the contract for the Webbs Hole Sluice Works.

4.7 The Owners shall be entitled to issue the Certificate of Practical Completion if after the expiry of 40 Working Days after the invitation made pursuant to paragraph 4.6 of this Schedule 16 if any of the following apply:

4.7.1 the Surveyor has failed to inspect the Webbs Hole Sluice Works; or

4.7.2 the Surveyor has failed to notify the Owners in writing of any Defects such notification having been received by the Owners within the said 40 Working Days; or

4.7.3 the Surveyor has indicated following an inspection there are no Defects to remedy; or

4.7.4 where the Surveyor (acting reasonably) has identified any Defects and where all such Defects have been properly remedied to the Surveyor's reasonable satisfaction provided that the provisions of clause 17 shall apply in the event of dispute as to:

* (a) whether any Defects identified by the Surveyor are reasonable and should be remedied or not; and/or

* (b) whether the Defects have been properly remedied.

4.8 The Owners shall send the Surveyor copies of all instructions issued to a contractor in respect of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works at the same time as issuing them and a copy of any Certificate of Practical Completion, list of Defects or Certificate of Final Completion issued in respect of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works within five Working Days of its receipt by the Owners AND for the avoidance of doubt the Owners shall obtain a Certificate of Practical Completion and Certificate of Final Completion themselves and these certificates will not be issued by the Surveyor SAVE THAT the Owners shall have complied with the provisions of paragraph 4.7 above in relation to the issue of the Certificate of Practical Completion only.

4.9 Notwithstanding any Certificate of Practical Completion:

4.9.1 the Owners shall be responsible for rectification of any and all material Defects which manifest themselves during a period of 12 months after the issue of a Certificate of Practical Completion is issued and thereafter (subject to the law relating to latent defects) the Owners shall procure that a Certificate of Final Completion is obtained, and

4.9.2 after the end of the 12 months period as aforesaid and before any Certificate of Final Completion is issued the Owners will procure an independent surveyor's report addressed to SCDC from a reputable consultant (and which Consultant will enter into a collateral warranty in favour of SCDC or such Alternative Body nominated by SCDC) and the Owners will take account of and rectify any and all Defects set out by such surveyors report.

4.10 The Owners shall use Reasonable Endeavours to procure that with effect from the Certificate of Practical Completion for the Webbs Hole Sluice Works the benefit of all guarantees and warranties relating to the design and construction of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works are assigned or otherwise, passed on to as applicable:

4.10.1 SCDC; or

4.10.2 such Alternative Body nominated by SCDC

PROVIDED THAT in the event of an insolvency Event on the part of the designers or contractors of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works such that the Owners having used Reasonable Endeavours have not obtained assignment or otherwise of all guarantees and warranties relating to the design and/or construction of the Webbs Hole Sluice Works the Owners shall obtain the necessary insurance for the benefit of SCDC or such Alternative Body nominated by SCDC in relation to defective construction and/or defective design as may be available from commercial insurers at reasonably commercial rates and terms for a insured term of not more than the period that the warranty or guarantee would otherwise endure for and on such insurance being put in place the obligation to obtain guarantees and warranties will cease to apply.

# SCHEDULE 17 - COVENANTS BY SCDC

1. APPLICATION OF FUNDS

SCDC covenants with the Owners that it will:

1.1 Keep account of interest attributable to all of the Contributions received by it under this Agreement:

1.2 In the event that upon the expiration of ten years (unless otherwise expressly stated below) after the date of receipt by SCDC of any Contribution or any part of any Contribution (together with any monies paid alongside the Contribution attributable to Indexation) then subject to the Overall Costs Cap having been expended by or on behalf of the Owners if there should be any unexpended balance (and/or which is not committed to be spent pursuant to a contract entered into prior to such date) SCDC shall retain the said unexpended balance and shall thereafter comply with the provisions of clauses 29.3 and 29.4.

1.3 Work with the Owners in order to use all Reasonable Endeavours to secure available and appropriate funding grants credits and benefits in respect of any SCDC Matters pursuant to this Agreement in respect of the Development and subject to the provisions of clause 29 and the Overall Costs Cap to allocate any such funding grants credits and benefits obtained by SCDC as a priority to the benefit of the Development PROVIDED ALWAYS that the spending of any such funding grants credit and benefits obtained by SCDC pursuant to this clause 1.3 shall not reduce the Owners' liability as to the aggregate Overall Cost Cap until the overall savings in respect of the Contributions and Works in Kind payable pursuant to this Agreement total at least £3,275,500 before Indexation).

1.4 Apply use and expend any Contributions (together with any monies paid alongside the Contribution attributable to Indexation and any interest accrued on the Contributions) it receives in an efficient and prudent way only towards the purposes for which they have been paid pursuant to the provisions of this Agreement (and which for the avoidance of doubt may include use towards any purposes pursuant to clause 29.4) with the overriding aim and commitment by SCDC to the Owners that subject to clause 29 they shall apply use and expend any Contributions so far as are reasonably necessary to fulfil the specified purposes of this Agreement so as to create the most reasonably beneficial circumstances for a Clawback Contribution to arise subject to clause 1.7 of this Schedule 17.

1.5 In the event that SCDC construct the Community Building and/or Sports Pavilion as provided for by Schedule 8 of this Agreement then upon the Community Building and/or Sports Pavilion (as applicable) being open for use by the general public as provided for in Schedule 8 of this Agreement then should there be any unexpended balance (and/or which is not committed to be spent pursuant to a contract entered into prior to such date) of all or any part of the Contribution SCDC retain the said unexpended balance and shall thereafter comply with the provisions of clauses 29.3 and 29.4.

1.5 In the event that SCDC construct the Webbs Hole Sluice Works as provided for by Schedule 16 of this Agreement then upon the Webbs Hole Sluice Works being operational as provided for in Schedule 16 of this Agreement then subject to the Overall Costs Cap should there be any unexpended balance (and/or which is not committed to be spent pursuant to a contract entered into prior to such date) of all or any part of the Webbs Hole Sluice Contribution and subject to the Overall Costs Cap having been expended by or on behalf of the Owners SCDC retain the said unexpended balance and shall thereafter comply with the provisions of clauses 29.3 and 29.4.
1.7 In accordance with clause 29.4.3 and together with the County Council to produce and serve the Owners with:

1.7.1 written notification every six calendar months commencing two years after Commencement of Development confirming (i) the amount of Clawback Contributions (if any) they hold pursuant to clause 29.4.1 and which notification shall also identify the Contribution that the Clawback Contribution originated from and the amount of that original Contribution which remains unexpended and (ii) where any Clawback Contribution has been expended to discharge any Remaining Obligations the notification shall identify which Remaining Obligation(s) has been so discharged; and

1.7.2 written notification within 40 Working Days of the date when the last Remaining Obligation has been performed or discharged PROVIDED ALWAYS that for the purposes of clause 29.4.3(i) the Remaining Obligations shall not include any On Site SUDS Contribution payable to the County Council pursuant to paragraph 3.6.4 of Schedule 4 of this Agreement; and

1.7.3 in the event that the On Site SUDs Contribution is payable to the County Council pursuant to paragraph 3.6.4 of Schedule 4 a final written notification pursuant to this clause 29.4.3 within 40 Working Days

1.8 In the event that a Clawback Contribution is payable pursuant to clause 29.4 then subject to

1.8.1 the overall savings in respect of the Contributions and Works in Kind pursuant to this Agreement totalling at least £3,275,500 before Indexation; and

1.8.2 the Remaining Obligations having been discharged in full,

then SCDC shall repay the Clawback Contribution to the payor of the relevant Clawback Contribution as follows:

1.8.3 within 20 Working Days of service of the written notification in accordance with clause 29.4.3.1 SCDC and the County Council shall pay any Clawback Contribution less the sum of any On Site SUDS Contribution remaining to be paid pursuant to this Agreement; and

1.8.4 within 20 Working Days of service of the written notification in accordance with clause 29.4.3.2 SCDC and the County Council shall pay any Clawback Contribution.

2. COMMUNITY

2.1 Subject to paragraph 1.4 of this schedule 17 SCDC shall only use the Community Support Contribution for the purposes identified in Schedule 10 and specifically will take the following steps in doing so:

2.1.1 to provide a development plan for the management and programming of community development activities within three months of the commencement of construction of the Community Building; and

2.1.2 to use Reasonable Endeavours to have a dedicated community development worker on a minimum of 20 hours per week in post not less than 9 months prior to the anticipated opening of the Community Building and to continue to employ such community development worker for up to 3 years or until the expenditure in full of the Community Support Contribution in accordance with this Schedule, whichever is the sooner and the
community development worker's role and responsibilities shall include
working with a variety of organisations including local Parish Councils and
when constituted the Northstowe Town Council to support and deliver
community and youth development work and to develop an infrastructure of
local community and youth groups to benefit the Development and to
establish and strengthen the community of Northstowe.

3. GOVERNANCE

3.1 SCDC covenants with the Owners that it will use Reasonable Endeavours to
establish/approve a Town Council for Northstowe as soon as reasonably practicable
following Commencement if it has not already taken steps to establish/approve a
Town Council in order that the Town Council is functioning and operational to the
extent it can accept transfers of any land and/or buildings as may be provided for
under this Agreement and thereafter has the necessary powers to appoint engage
instruct contractors employees agents consultants or other bodies organisations or
individuals to manage and maintain any land or buildings that the Town Council
accepts a transfer of under the terms of this Agreement.

4. STATUTORY AND OTHER CONSENTS AND ORDERS REQUIRED

4.1 SCDC will use Reasonable Endeavours to co-operate with the Highways Agency and
any other statutory parties in connection with any aspect of the Development which
involves the assistance consent or co-operation of the Highways Agency or other
statutory parties PROVIDED ALWAYS THAT SCDC will not be required to spend any
monies whether capital or revenue in so doing but will ensure that reasonable officer
time is allocated from time to time.

5. LAND

5.1 SCDC will use Reasonable Endeavours to co-operate with the Owners in the grant of
any rights, approvals and consents in respect of land (if any) owned by SCDC as
reasonably required by the Owners to comply with the obligations in the Agreement
PROVIDED ALWAYS THAT SCDC will not be required to spend any monies whether
capital or revenue in so doing but will ensure that reasonable officer time is allocated
from time to time.

5.2 In the event that SCDC accepts an offer made to have transferred to it the Community
Building Land and/or the Allotment Land and/or Sports Pavilion Land and/or any part
of the Open Space Land then:

5.2.1 SCDC shall if it accepts the transfer will do so subject to the terms as set out
within the relevant Schedule of this Agreement; and

5.2.2 SCDC shall execute deliver and complete the relevant transfer to the
Owners as soon as reasonably practicable in relation to or having regard to
the relevant trigger.
SCHEDULE 18 - COVENANTS BY THE COUNTY COUNCIL

1.  APPLICATION OF FUNDS
    The County Council covenants with the Owners that it will

1.1 keep account of interest received by it under this Agreement,

1.2 In the event that upon the Primary School Building being capable of being open for use by pupils as provided for in Schedule 2 of this Agreement there should be any unexpended balance (and/or which is not committed to be spent pursuant to a building design contribution then subject to the Overall Costs Cap having been expended by or on behalf of the Owners the County Council shall retain the said unexpended balance and shall thereafter comply with the provisions of clauses 29.3 and 29.4;

1.3 In the event that upon the expiration of five years after the date of receipt by the County Council of all or any part of the following Contributions (together with any monies paid alongside the Contribution attributable to Indexation) (then subject to the Overall Costs Cap having been expended by or on behalf of the Owners) there should be any unexpended balance (and/or which is not committed to be spent pursuant to a contract entered into prior to such date) the County Council shall retain the said unexpended balance and shall thereafter comply with the provisions of clauses 29.3 and 29:
    1.3.1 Offsite Footpath and Cycleway Contribution;
    1.3.2 the Primary School Building Construction Contribution

1.4 In the event that upon the expiration of ten years after the date of receipt by the County Council of all or any part of the following contributions (together with any monies paid alongside the Contribution attributable to Indexation) there should be any unexpended balance (and/or which is not committed to be spent pursuant to a contract entered into prior to such date) then subject to the Overall Costs Cap having been expended by or on behalf of the Owners the County Council shall retain the said unexpended balance and shall thereafter comply with the provisions of clauses 29.3 and 29.4:
    1.4.1 Secondary School Design Contribution;
    1.4.2 Secondary School Construction Contribution
    1.4.3 Household Waste Recycling Centre Contribution
    1.4.4 On-Site SUDs Contribution
    1.4.5 CGB Capital Funding Contribution
    1.4.6 Bus Funding Contribution
    1.4.7 Community Transport Contribution
    1.4.8 Capital Transport Monitoring Contribution
    1.4.9 Annual Transport Monitoring Contribution
    1.4.10 Busway Link Road Contribution
1.5 Work with the Owners to use all Reasonable Endeavours to in order to secure all
available and appropriate funding grants credits and benefits in respect of any County
Matters pursuant to this Agreement in respect of the Development which funding
grants credit and benefits obtained by the County Council shall subject as hereinafter
provided be allocated and used by the County Council to the benefit of the
Development PROVIDED ALWAYS that the spending of any such funding grants
credit and benefits obtained by the County Council pursuant to this clause 1.5 shall
not reduce the Owners' liability as to the aggregate Overall Cost Cap until the overall
savings in respect of the Contributions and Works in Kind payable pursuant to this
Agreement total at least £3,275,500 before Indexation);

1.6 To only draw against any Bonds that have been provided by the Owners to the County
Council's benefit pursuant to the terms of this Agreement in the event of default by the
Owners of the bonded obligations and to comply with the terms of the Bond in relation
to drawing against the Bond and discharging it in full or in part.

1.7 Apply use and expend any Contributions (together with any monies paid alongside the
Contribution attributable to Indexation and any interest accrued on the Contributions) it
receives in an efficient and prudent way to only towards the purposes for which they
have been paid pursuant to the provisions of this Agreement (and which for the
avoidance of doubt may include use towards any purposes pursuant to clause 29.4)
with the overriding aims and commitments by the County Council to the Owners that
subject to clause 29 they shall only apply use and expend any Contributions so far as
are reasonably necessary to fulfil the specified purpose so as to create the most
reasonably beneficial circumstances for a Clawback Contribution to arise subject to
paragraph 1.8 of this Schedule 18.

1.8 In accordance with clause 29.4.3 and together with SCDC to produce and serve the
Owners with:

1.8.1 written notification every six calendar months commencing two years after
Commencement of Development confirming (i) the amount of Clawback
Contributions (if any) they hold pursuant to clause 29.4.1 and which
notification shall also identify the Contribution that the Clawback Contribution
originated from and the amount of that original Contribution which remains
unexpended and (ii) where any Clawback Contribution has been expended
to discharge any Remaining Obligations the notification shall identify which
Remaining Obligation(s) has been so discharged; and

1.8.2 written notification within 40 Working Days of the date when the last
Remaining Obligation has been performed or discharged PROVIDED
ALWAYS that for the purposes of clause 29.4.3 (i) the Remaining
Obligations shall not include any On Site SUDS Contribution payable to the
County Council pursuant to paragraph 3.6.4 of Schedule 4 of this
Agreement; and

1.8.3 in the event that the On Site SUDs Contribution is payable to the County
Council pursuant to paragraph 3.6.4 of Schedule 4 a final written notification
to this clause 29.4.3 within 40 Working Days

1.9 In the event that a Clawback Contribution is payable pursuant to clause 29.4 then
subject to:

1.9.1 the overall savings in respect of the Contributions and Works in Kind
pursuant to this Agreement totalling at least £3,275,500 before Indexation;
and

1.9.2 the Remaining Obligations having been discharged in full;
then the County Council shall repay the Clawback Contribution to the payor of the relevant Clawback Contribution as follows:

1.9.3 within 20 Working Days of service of the written notification in accordance with clause 29.4.3.1 SCDC and the County Council shall pay any Clawback Contribution less the sum of any On Site SUDS Contribution remaining to be paid pursuant to this Agreement; and

1.9.4 within 20 Working Days of service of the written notification in accordance with clause 29.4.3.2 SCDC and the County Council shall pay any Clawback Contribution.

## 2. EDUCATION

2.1 In respect of the planning obligations contained in Schedule 2 the County Council additionally covenants as follows:

2.1.1 the County Council covenants with the Owners not to use the Primary School Building Design Contribution and Primary School Building Construction Contribution received from the Owners pursuant to Schedule 2 towards any costs or fees associated with delivering a Primary School Building that is larger or is delivered to a higher specification than that approved as part of the Project Core Team meetings as necessary to mitigate the impact of the Development;

2.1.2 as soon as possible following the grant of the Planning Permission to apply for and use reasonable endeavours to obtain prudential borrowing from the Public Works Loan Board or such other alternative funding source as may be notified in writing to the Owners in order to secure the necessary funds to forward fund the cost of design and construction of the Primary School Building;

2.1.3 to only drawdown from the prudential borrowing available to the County Council secured pursuant to paragraph 2.1.2 of this Schedule 18 above such monies as are immediately required at the time of drawdown to fund the stage of design or construction of the Primary School Building that the County Council is at AND PROVIDED THAT for the avoidance of doubt the County Council shall not drawdown the full extent of the borrowing available for the design and construction of Primary School Building in one lump sum nor for works or other actions and activities connected to the design and construction of the Primary School Building that may occur more than 40 Working Days in advance of the date of drawdown;

2.1.4 to use the monies received from the Owners pursuant to paragraphs 2.3 and 2.5 of Part 2 of Schedule 2 to immediately (time being of the essence in relation to this paragraph) and no later than 20 Working Days after receipt or deemed receipt of any instalment of the said monies repay any capital element of the prudential borrowing loan that has been secured through the Public Works Loan Board to forward fund the provisions of the Primary School Building such that the Owners shall not be adversely affected by delays in repayment which will result in additional annual interest charges. To only use the payments received pursuant to paragraph 15 of Part 2 to discharge any interest payments due to the loan company in relation to the borrowing referred to above PROVIDED THAT where the County Council have already paid the annual interest payments to the loan company at the point they receive the payment from the Owners representing the said interest payments for the preceding 12 month period then the County Council shall be entitled to retain such monies received from the Owners,

2.1.5 to use the payments received pursuant to paragraph 2 and 5 of Part 3 of Schedule 2 towards funding the cost of the Secondary School Building.

## 3. STATUTORY AND OTHER CONSENTS AND ORDERS REQUIRED
3.1 The County Council will co-operate with the Highways Agency and any other statutory parties in connection with any aspect of the Development which involves the assistance consent or co-operation of the Highways Agency or other statutory parties.

## 4. LAND
4.1 The County Council will use all Reasonable Endeavours to co-operate with the Owners in the grant of any rights, approvals and consents in respect of land owned by the County Council as reasonably required by the Owners to comply with the obligations in the Agreement PROVIDED ALWAYS THAT the County Council will not be required to spend any monies whether capital or revenue in so doing but will ensure that reasonable officer time is allocated from time to time.

4.2 The County Council shall use Reasonable Endeavours to respond in writing to any offer to transfer land pursuant to this Agreement within 60 Working Days.

4.3 In the event that the County Council accepts the offer made to have transferred to it the Primary School Land, the Household Waste Recycling Centre Land, the On-Site SUDS Land or the Off Site Flood Mitigation Land then:

4.3.1 the County Council shall use Reasonable Endeavours to agree the final transfer within three months of the date of the offer having been made; and

4.3.2 the County Council shall accept the transfer on the terms as set out within the relevant Schedule of this Agreement; and

4.3.3 the County Council shall execute and deliver the relevant transfer to the Owners with time being of the essence.

4.4 In respect of any land transferred to the County Council pursuant to the terms of this Agreement the County Council covenants to use the land solely for the purpose for which the land has been transferred.

4.5 In relation to the Primary School Building and Household Waste Recycling Centre the County Council covenants with the Owners to use Reasonable Endeavours to efficiently design, layout and construct the Primary School Building and Household Waste Recycling Centre including any use of the Primary School Building Land and Household Waste Recycling Centre Land which does not involve the construction of building(s) with the overriding aim and commitment to the Owners that the County Council shall only use such part of the Primary School Building Land and Household Waste Recycling Centre Land as is absolutely necessary to deliver the Primary School Building and Household Waste Recycling Centre to applicable building standards and in accordance with the Detailed Primary School Building Works Specification and Household Recycling Specification (as applicable) AND PROVIDED THAT in the event any part of the Primary School Building Land and/or Household Waste Recycling Centre Land is not required for the construction of the Primary School Building and Household Waste Recycling Centre (respectively) then the County Council covenants with the Owners to offer to transfer such land back to Gallagher for nil consideration and if Gallagher accepts such an offer the County Council shall transfer as soon as practicably possible with each party bearing their own legal costs and there being no restrictions contained in the transfer on the future to put such land to together with such other terms restrictions and rights as may be agreed between the parties.

## 5. COMMUNITY ACCESS TO THE PRIMARY SCHOOL

5.1 The County Council covenants with SCDC that (subject to having first liaised and agreed with SCDC the relevant details) the County Council shall as soon as reasonably possible agree in writing with the School Promoter those parts of the Primary School that shall be made available for local community use capable of serving residents of the Development and those working or otherwise associated with the Development and such use to be:

5.1.1 at reasonable times outside normal school hours during term times and including evenings and week-ends; and

5.1.2 at reasonable times including day-time and evenings and at week-ends during school holidays; and

5.1.3 at reasonable times during normal school hours and during term times in relation to those specific parts of the primary School which are agreed as (i) intended for dedicated local community use and (ii) including any rooms to be identified for any such community use; and

5.1.4 for the period between the date when the Primary School Building is open to pupils and the date when the Community Building is open to the public

and provided always that (i) such arrangements do not prejudice the operation of the education elements of the Primary School itself and any statutory duties placed on the County Council under the Education Acts and the details (ii) set out any contribution to be made by the parties towards the associated costs arising from such access arrangement and details of which are to be set out in a Community Access Agreement to be agreed and entered into by the Councils and the School Promoter (and the health service provider (if relevant) and such Agreement to be entered into before any works take place to the fitting out of the Primary School Building

# APPENDIX 16 – INITIAL COMMUNITY BUILDING SPECIFICATION

## 1. Purpose of the document

1.1 This document is appended to the Section 106 relating to the development of Phase 1 of the new town of Northstowe.

1.2 It should be read in conjunction with the provisions set out in Schedule 8.

1.3 It forms the agreed basis, subject to any variations that SCDC has notified to Gallagher in writing on which further collaborative work on the Community Building will be undertaken. In particular the Community Building Detailed Specification will be based upon the contents of this document and provide further details on each of the building elements set out below. Together with any related drawings, the Community Building Detailed Specification will be a binding part of the Building Contract and will be the construction specification for the works.

## 2. Context

2.1 South Cambridgeshire District Council and its partner Cambridgeshire County Council are committed to ensuring the provision of the Community Centre in Phase 1 of the Northstowe plans.

2.2 It is essential that this Centre provides accommodation for early delivery of core community services and that it provides a place for the (new) residents to meet informally to begin to form essential social support and community networks.

2.3 Multi-purpose and multi-functional, the Centre must facilitate the early development of a thriving community and, as such, the spaces must be flexible and adaptable in order to meet a wide range of community needs and activities.

2.4 The design of the Centre should also be innovative and creative as it will play a key role in supporting the early development of a sense of place and identity for Northstowe. It will be a central focus for the first Local Centre and its community. Its design will accord with the requirements of the Phase 1 Design Code once approved and will be considered by the Cambridgeshire Quality Panel.

2.5 The development of a mixed use shared facility is one of the key County and District Council policy principles established to guide the provision of community services and facilities in the new community.

2.6 It has been established that community, learning, cultural, heritage and leisure services will make a positive, creative contribution to the vitality and viability of the new settlements in Cambridgeshire, playing a crucial role in their development, cohesion, sense of place, quality of life and sustainability.

2.7 The customer focus and long term sustainability of the services and facilities will best be assured by establishing joint delivery arrangements wherever possible, with service provision co-located as part of multi-service / multi-agency provision.

2.8 The early integration of sports and arts facilities within the Community Centre is an important element of community provision bringing added value to the town as a whole. The provision will facilitate the solid foundation for a sustainable, diverse, inclusive creative and cohesive community.

2.9 The spatial requirements for public assembly and celebration, performing and rehearsing arts/creating arts and entertainments need to address the commercial needs for a reasonable sized and properly equipped space, a community need to undertake a range of activities, to be sustainable and have long term viability but also need to provide a space that is as large, adaptable and as flexible as reasonably possible.

2.10 Provision should be made for the viewing of the sports halls and other activity areas if possible as this provides added interest to the social content and assists in breaking down the perceived cellular characteristics common to many sports buildings.

**3. The Site and Building**

3.1 The proposed location of the Community Building is shown on Plan [7] attached to the S106. This shows the Community Building in one of two parcels which together comprise the Local Centre. Additional information in the approved Design & Access Statement (February 2012) and the draft Design Code explain the siting of the building.

3.2 The definition of the Community Building in the S106 confirms that the internal floor space is 950sq.m. The building will be designed as a multi-use, flexible community facility accommodating a range of temporary and permanent uses including meetings, functions, recreation and play, health and library services.

3.3 It is intended that part of the building will be 2 storey

3.4 Outside of the building there will be associated servicing, car parking, landscaping and amenity space. A decision will be taken at a later date whether an Informal Single Court MUGA will also be constructed adjacent to the building.

## 4. Accommodation

4.1 The First Community Centre is required to provide space and facilities for the following key functions:

### Foyer / Reception
- **Use**: Entrance, reception, waiting and greeting area
- **Size m²**: 40

**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**: Reception desk/window into office, seating, notice boards, main entrance doors, public telephone, direct access to toilets

### Main Hall with stage
- **Use**: Multi-purpose hall to accommodate sport and arts use
- **Size m²**: 300

**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**: The interior must be designed to be a suitable multi-functional environment for all potential uses, including for sporting and nonsporting events, public assembly, music, drama, dance, film etc. visual arts, performance, performing arts, practice and tuition, lectures and demonstrations; with lighting that can be adapted to suit the different activities. The main activity spaces to conform to recognised sports dimensions set out by Sport England as a two-court hall 18 x 17 (17.6 x 16.7 min) x 6.1–7.6m clear height, dimension and height to accommodate badminton.

A safe and durable floor finish that is also suitable for badminton use.

The hall finishes are to be specified to withstand games practice with soft balls only to ensure the hall is suitable for nonsporting use.

An audience capacity for amateur dramatic productions and large public meetings in the region of 350.

The requirement for a stage is to be met by the use of a modular platform that can create a stage of the required size in the required position but which will require to be stored in close proximity to the hall.

Stage to have curtains, stage lighting and ceiling height to accommodate suitable stage lighting and sound systems / data / overhead projector / screen and associated storage, lighting suitable for performance and sports use.

Dressing room (male and female) including showers.

Fold-out partitioning.

Adequate storage cupboards for hall users are to be provided and are to be accessible from the spaces they serve.

### Second Hall
- **Use**: Smaller multi-purpose hall for meetings, classes and general community use
- **Size m²**: 100

**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**: Sprung floor for dance studio use, mirrored walls, audio visual equipment including screen, storage / folding tables / stacking chairs / computer facilities

### 2 meeting rooms
- **Use**: Multi-purpose spaces for meetings and group activities
- **Size m²**: 64

**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**: Two multi-functional meeting areas are required for the use of the building occupants and the community. The rooms are to be accessible from the main circulation areas and will require visual and acoustic privacy. The uses will include meetings of a private and confidential nature as well as for general use. The rooms will be available for use by statutory bodies such as the Police or District Council as well as clubs and societies. The rooms will operate a reservation system run by the centre management.

One to have carpeting, curtains, audio visual and lighting equipment for use as drama rehearsal space, folding tables, computer facilities / stacking chairs / white board / data / power.

### Community Lounge / Café
- **Use**: Meeting and informal Community gathering space, serving hot drinks / snacks
- **Size m²**: 100

**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**:
The café will be a shared facility open to the public and specific building users. It should be located close to the entrance foyer to enhance the welcoming ambience and to enable the centre to be used as a meeting place and should be designed to ensure that standards of décor match successful high street equivalents.

A durable good quality flooring should be provided to the café area

Informal seating / tables / chairs plus vending machines for out of hours.

Space for the community library service to operate including folding secure book stores is to be provided (e.g. Gamlingay).

Computer area for 6 terminals, carpeted in parts e.g. the library area.

### Kitchen / Store
- **Use**: Small commercial standard kitchen to serve the main hall and café
- **Size m²**: 50

**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**:The primary function of the kitchen will be to service the centre café / provide catering for meetings / events private parties using the centre. It should be located to directly serve the café and the main hall to offer full service flexibility.

Whenever possible the kitchen should be located on an external wall, easing delivery and service requirements.

Usual requirements are for a small commercial / industrial kitchen to EHO standards equipped with four ring cooker, double sink, fridge, freezer, microwave oven, a dish washer, work tops, storage, lighting, and ventilation. A separate wash hand basin must also be included.

The facility will be available for hire where private caters are used for receptions and private parties.

### Centre Office with first aid room
- **Use**: A shared office for use by centre staff and visiting community workers
- **Size m²**: 40

**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**: The main centre office is to be located in close proximity to the reception, foyer, main entrance and main circulation spaces, with window into reception area.

It will be a shared facility for building occupants as a point of information and management.

The office will offer a base for staff, a reception and bookings point, a records store and a location for the master service control for the centre and will provide touch down desks for visiting community workers including police. 5 No office desks and chairs, storage including secure storage for confidential records, ICT provision, fixtures and fittings to be discussed, kitchenette for staff use.

The room should be naturally ventilated with daylight and should overlook the main approach to the building but will require secure storage for confidential records.

First aid room off the office.

### Cleaner's Store
**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**: To be provided located close to the changing accommodation and include a bucket sink and storage spaces and shelves.

### Storage
- **Use**: Storage space for push chairs
- **Size m²**: 100

**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**: Shelving and storage against walls.

### Toilets
- **Use**: Male, Female and Disabled toilets and nappy changing facilities
- **Size m²**: 45

**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**: Male, female and disabled people's toilets are to be provided close to the entrance foyer using hard wearing easily cleaned materials with suitable vandal proof fittings and fixtures and including electric hand dryers. A fully equipped baby changing room is also to be provided with access from the general circulation areas. Min 2 WC's and 2 urinals in male and 3 WC's in female with basins, electric hand dryers, mirrors and handbag shelf.

### Changing areas
- **Use**: Male and female for staff use plus use by the community for performances
- **Size m²**: 25

**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**: Well-designed changing rooms using robust materials and fittings which are easy to clean and maintain and sized to reflect general community / building user's needs. The location of the changing rooms should enable them to serve what would be a back stage areas for performances. The size and number of facilities within changing rooms should relate to the normal maximum occupancy level of the activity spaces they support but each should be large enough for 12 adults to change and should be adjacent to the main toilet facilities. Two shower cubicles should be provided in each changing room. Separate toilet, changing and shower facilities are required to be provided for disabled people, with 8 half height lockers for staff use plus standard benches and clothes hooks

### Plant
- **Use**: Heating plant and electrical switch boards plus water storage
- **Size m²**: 9

**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**: An efficient, economic energy efficient heating system suitable for both space heating and for heating hot water is to be installed using the most sustainable heat source. The heating system should be fully controlled to ensure it meets the needs of the users. The use of heat transfer technology should be included and all will be designed to meet the Passivhaus standard. A ground sourced heat pump system should be incorporated into the building. Solar panels should be utilised. The plant room should include gas, electric and water meters, switch and control gear plus an IT server and communications hub will be required to be secure. Windows and ventilators to provide efficient natural ventilation with adequate privacy and security are to be installed to provide controlled cross-ventilation throughout the building. Natural ventilation, with de-humidification, should be used wherever possible but kitchens, toilets, changing rooms and showers must have mechanical extraction.

### Lighting
**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**: A presence detection system should be used throughout the building for all primary light sources. Time clock or sensor control will be required for external illumination. The main and small halls will need an overall lighting system and will also require secondary decorative and specialist lighting. When it will be used for drama productions ensure the hall is to be equipped with power points located for temporary stage lighting installation. Emergency lighting will be required and all will be designed to meet the Passivhaus standard.

### Protection and Fire Safety
**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**: Fire escape doors should have recessed panic push bars or flush mounted push pads to meet all statutory requirements. Fire extinguishers are to be provided and should be located or recessed to minimize obstruction and possible damage. Security alarm, smoke detectors and a fire alarm system are to be installed with a remote link to a call centre. Lightning protection will be required to be installed.

### Circulation 7.5%
- **Use**: General movement spaces for the public
- **Size m²**: 72

**Features / Requirements / Access / Fitout**: A simple, economical and spacious circulation system that is clearly intelligible to the user and permits easy supervision to allow safe and secure access. All levels must be capable of being accessed by the disabled user. Servicing and plant room access should be remote from the main entrance but located as close as possible to the most heavily serviced spaces. Hard wearing but practical, using attractive materials and lighting

**Total:** 945m²

## 5. Quality and Specification standards
### 5.1 Design Principles

5.2 The building will form a focus for the emerging community in the northern area of Northstowe. As a landmark in the Local Centre it will need to be easily identifiable and contribute to a sense of place. The choice of massing, external materials, height, and degree of innovation will be guided by the Design Code.

5.3 Accessibility must accommodate all users and abilities, including older persons and special needs users.

5.4 The layout of the proposed functions of the building must achieve an efficient and flexible plan form. Routes through the building should allow for reasonable segregation of user groups, physically, visually and acoustically.

5.5 The key functional areas of the Centre will share a common reception and have access to common facilities such as café, kitchen, changing rooms, meeting rooms and interview rooms.

5.6 The room sizes are indicative and subject to variation to address the client's needs. They will be confirmed at detailed specification stage.

5.7 The materials choice should be robust throughout, especially for doors which are subject to high levels of use and potential abuse.

5.8 The building should be constructed of traditional external materials, with the building exterior attaining a minimum height of 10m.

5.9 Construction techniques should be used that will enable future conversion to accommodate changing uses.

5.10 A security alarm should be provided.

5.11 A lift adequate for general use should be provided to allow people and equipment to be transported between the ground and first floors.

5.12 A broadband connection should be provided and connected for use within the facility at all locations within the building where broadband access may be required.

## 5.13 Sustainability

5.14 The environmental aspirations are to Exemplar quality creating a facility with low energy requirements and generate low levels of carbon emissions. BREEAM Excellent should be the delivered standard, or following PassiveHaus principles. To achieve the PassiveHaus standard the energy required for space heating must not exceed 15 kWh/(m2 a). In addition to this the total primary energy demand must not exceed 120 kWh/(m2 a).

* Super-insulation: opaque U-values must be less than 0.15 W/m2K
* U-values for windows and doors need to be 0.8 W/m2K or less (for both the frame and glazing). This requires the window frame to incorporate insulation and triple glazing
* Thermal bridging needs to be minimised, and ideally eliminated
* Airtightness: n50 of 0.6 h-1 @ 50 Pa or less
* Whole building mechanical ventilation with heat recovery (75% efficient or better, with a low specific fan power).
* Solar thermal panels should also be provided to preheat water reducing the requirement for heating water.
* Utilisation of passive solar gains to reduce space heating and day-lighting requirements
* Internal gains and overheating.
* Renewable and low-carbon technologies. Ground sourced heat pumps should be used to provide additional heating requirements.

## 5.15 Car parking and external areas

5.16 External amenity spaces including external seating are to be provided with access directly from the public spaces within the facility.

5.17 A dedicated shielded space for the storage of refuse bins that is safe secure and convenient.

5.18 Public car parking will be provided in an adjacent public area but a minimum of 10 standard parking bays are required plus 5 specifically for the disabled located close to the main entrance.

5.19 External areas are to be well-lit for safe access after dark.

5.20 Bicycle lock-up parking close to the entrance where it can be overseen is also required.

## 5.21 Access

5.22 The community buildings will generate significant traffic and can be used to assist in improving footfalls to local shops and other amenities. A level approach to the entrance from the public footpaths to enable safe and convenient access for people with pushchairs or
wheelchairs and for deliveries to the building is required. A visually strong entrance is
required to provide legibility.

5.23 If funding permits CCTV should be provided with PIR lighting, flood lighting, etc. An
external notice board for the display of useful information and events should be provided.

5.24 Reception / Common Areas
5.25 The design of the Foyer/ Reception and entrance areas must be warm and welcoming.

5.26 The layout of the foyer should be clear and open with associated seating, meeting,
viewing and refreshment areas.

5.27
* Sight lines must be clear to key functions of the building.
* The lift, staircase and public telephone should be visible and close to the reception
desk.
* Location of the reception desk/window.
* Suitable seating for early arrivals and people who may be meeting friends before or
after using the facilities.
* The reception area should be acoustically quiet so that communication, particularly
with staff at the reception desk/window, is clear and free of distractions.
* There is a dedicated space for the provision of local public transport and taxi
information along with a notice board for events.
* A convenient and secure store for pushchairs overlooked from reception
* Automatic operation of the main doors to help disabled users and people with young
children is required but the design must prevent heat loss through the open doors.

## 6. External Sports Areas

6.1 The main sports facilities including pitches, the formal floodlit MUGA will be located in
the open space which forms a buffer between the new development and Longstanton and
will have a sports pavilion provided to meet the needs for changing, social and other
facilities.

6.2 There is however the need to provide an informal play area close to the Community
Centre.

6.3 The Ball Court or “informal” MUGA will be approximately 700m² (35-37m x 18.5-20m) on
a macadam base and will be an enclosed area with galvanised steel uprights for rebound
and football goals and basketball posts at either end. The facility will be robust and vandal
proof and will meet BS EN 15312:2007.

6.4 The ball court should include floodlights lights to provide young people with a place to go
after school and during the winter months and with a youth shelter located adjacent to it.
This facility is to be located in an area where some informal surveillance from family homes,
community facilities, passing pedestrians and traffic will be possible.

7. Management

7.1 The adjacency and phasing of the development will have a key impact on the initial viability of the Community Centre. Early provision of community facilities will be made within the first primary school and by use of a temporary community house but as the population grows the floor space in the school will be required for children and the purpose built community centre will be required.

7.2 The Primary School, the Community Centre and the first Sports Hub will create a triangle of facilities which will form the basis of the community facilities and services being provided for first Phase of Northstowe.

7.3 The ownership and management arrangements for the community centre need to be confirmed at an early stage as this would be a key influence on the design and specification of the building but should provide a coordinated provision. The stakeholders will need to consider ultimate responsibility for day-to-day operation and facility management. It is envisaged that facilities management will be undertaken on a shared basis and may include the following elements:

* a) building and services maintenance
* b) building cleaning services
* c) caretaker and security services
* d) catering services
* e) courier services - copying and printing facilities should be available for the use of all departments
* f) environmental control
* g) fire equipment
* h) grounds maintenance services
* i) utilities and statutory charges
* j) waste storage/removal.

**8. Trigger points**
8.1 The trigger points will be those as set out in the S106 Agreement.

**9. Detailed Specification**
9.1 The detailed specification shall contain and address all the necessary specification requirements in relation to design, materials, legal, workmanship, performance standards etc. including the following:

A. Contractors Obligations
a. The Contractor is required to execute and complete the works for the Community Building and associated external works strictly in accordance with all Contract Documents.
b. The Form of Contract will be ACA Form of Building Agreement 1982 Third Edition 1998 (2003 Revision) or as otherwise agreed.

B. Statutory and Legal Requirements
a. Legislation

i. Planning

C. Liaison with the end user

D. Materials and Workmanship

c. Storage handling fixing protection in accordance with manufacturer's instructions and recommendations
d. Materials not to be used
e. Workmanship - Methods of Construction
i. Good Building Practice
ii. Health & Safety Precautions on site handling materials
iii. Temporary Works Method Statement

E. Design Criteria
a. Building Design Loads
b. Loads for Buildings

d. Rainfall
e. Roof
f. Gutters and Outlets
g. Utilities and Services

ii. Gas

F. Elemental Building Specification
