# APPENDIX 17 – INITIAL SPORTS PAVILION SPECIFICATION

## 1. Purpose of the document

1.1 This document is appended to the Section 106 relating to the development of Phase 1 of the new town of Northstowe.

1.2 It should be read in conjunction with the provisions set out in Schedule 8.

1.3 It forms the agreed basis, subject to any variations that SCDC has notified to Gallagher in writing, on which further collaborative work on the Sports Pavilion will be undertaken. In particular the Sports Pavilion Detailed Specification will be based upon the contents of this document and will provide further details on each of the building elements set out below. Together with any related drawings, the Sports Pavilion Detailed Specification will be a binding part of the Building Contract and will be the construction specification for the works.

## 2. Context
2.1 South Cambridgeshire District Council is committed to ensuring the provision of the Sports Pavilion in Phase 1 of the Northstowe plans.

2.2 The early delivery of sports facilities is an important element of community provision bringing added value to the town as a whole.

2.3 The proposals for sports provision for phase 1 are based on South Cambridgeshire District Council's adopted planning policy documents and the endorsed Development Framework Document. The intention is that the elements of the sports hub including the sports pavilion building will be built to the relevant Sport England standards.

2.4 The Phase 1 development represents 15% of the total development site in terms of total number of dwellings. As the Phase 1 area includes the bulk of one of three sports hubs for Northstowe, the proposal is broadly based on one third of the sports facilities being required. The sports hub will be provided in two phases spread over Phases 1 and 2 of the whole Northstowe development.

2.5 The total area provided on the western side within Phase 1 will be 6.17ha. This forms part of the green separation between Northstowe and the village of Longstanton. A further 1.77ha will be provided by the HCA in the subsequent phase of the town, which will complete this first sports hub.

## 3. Needs
3.1 It is essential that the first sports pavilion should provide:-
* Sufficient space for the changing facilities to Sport England standards as well as space for future expansion

- Adequate car parking provision, including the potential for overspill car parking. In total 99 car parking spaces will be required, of which six should be for disabled users. As the sports pitches are to be provided in two phases, two-thirds of the parking spaces or not less than 66 spaces including all the disabled spaces should be provided for the opening of the first phase of pitches, with the remainder provided for at the opening of the second phase. Sufficient land will be safeguarded in the Phase 1 land, adjoining or in the vicinity of the pavilion, in order to provide for the remaining spaces when the second phase of the sports pitches are constructed.
- Access for service and emergency vehicles
- An appropriate relationship with the sports pitches it will serve

## 4. Sports Pavilion

4.1 The Sport England technical standards for sport pavilions will be used to inform the design of the sport pavilion. It will be a multi-purpose building and will provide team and referee/umpire changing room provision for the pitches as well as allowing for the future incorporation of social facilities. The siting of the pavilion and car park including access will be considered at the detailed design and planning stage.

4.2 The scale and type of changing and social accommodation depends upon the number and types of pitches served as well as the different sports that will use the clubhouse. Ideally any entrances to the clubhouse should be orientated away from the direction of the prevailing wind and should not face the setting sun. The design of the pavilion should maximise the comfortable viewing of the principal sports pitches from the clubhouse.

4.3 The location, size and use of the changing rooms, toilets, showers and social club will be carefully considered alongside the location of pitches and other uses which will include servicing, car parking and access. Due to the varied nature of the facilities and users, there is a need for the careful arrangement of facilities to avoid conflict. The car parking for the demands of access to facilities and the relative location of pitches

4.4 Subject to the definition of the Sports Pavilion, the accommodation and floor area of the sports pavilion will be based on the following criteria
    - Minimum changing areas of 16 square metres per team for football, cricket and hockey
    - Minimum changing area of 20 square metres per team for rugby
    - 1 shower point per 4 changing spaces, 1 square metre per shower and 1 square metre for drying area
    - Corridors should be minimum 1.5m wide
    - Two individual team changing rooms for each senior pitch
    - Club room area equal to 20 square metres per changing room
    - Unisex team changing rooms with access to 2 WC's and 2 wash basins en suite
    - 3 Officials' changing spaces per senior pitch catering for male and female officials separately
    - Accessible changing area with shower and toilet for use by wheelchair users.

* The sports pavilion should be designed as a single story building to avoid problems with accessibility, duplication of facilities and the provision of a lift which would have a high annual maintenance cost. It should be designed to accommodate easily an extension at a later date to include social facilities.
* Changing room bench lengths of 500 millimetres per person.

**5. Provision / accommodation**

Some areas have been kept to a minimum on economic grounds but the design and layout of the building on the site should provide the option for expansion at a later date if required.

| Sports hub 1 including Social Club + 8 changing rooms | m² |
| :---------------------------------------------------- | :--- |
| Area                                                  |      |

| Office                                                | 10   |

| Changing Rooms including showers and officials (male and female) | 527  |

**6. Description of facilities.**

* Lobby / entrance and location for vending machines and public telephone
* Office. Small office with views of clubhouse main entrance, pitches and floodlit synthetic turf pitches
* Any future club room provision should have large windows for viewing the sports pitches and direct access to the bar and kitchen
* Provide the infrastructure for the future provision of a commercial grade kitchen serving the future club room and the outside
*   Changing rooms 6 No. individual football team, unisex changing rooms with one shower point for every 4 changing spaces, 2 WC's and 2 wash basins. 2 No. 20 m² individual rugby team changing rooms with one shower point for every 4 changing spaces, 2 WC's and 2 wash basins
*   Two Officials' self-contained changing rooms with capacity for three officials for each senior pitch. Two separate officials changing rooms to be provided so that both sexes can be accommodated at the same time.
*   Physiotherapy and first aid room with full length treatment table with access on three sides
*   Three kit bag stores with roller shutter door, or equivalent in terms of robustness and security (which may require external access), slatted shelving and safe area to be provided for each pair of changing rooms. This facilitates doubling up of teams on a single changing room and offers flexibility and increased capacity.
*   Secure locker storage for personal possessions and medical supplies. These rooms double up as accessible changing, shower and WC.
*   Furniture store so that club room floor can be cleared for events or to store indoor sports equipment
*   Corridors are to be at least 1.5 metres wide and provide convenient access to pitches. The layout is to be designed to enable direct access from changing rooms to the main entrance and club room via a dedicated corridor with secure lockers for individuals' valuables
*   Public toilets male, female and disabled, including baby changing, off foyer to avoid need to open up club room
*   Cleaner's cupboard and store, lockable cupboard with bucket sink and shelving
*   Plant room
*   Outdoor store storage for nets, corner posts, netball posts, mini soccer goals, balls, training equipment and maintenance equipment. 11-a-side goal posts will be left erected or stored elsewhere. It is assumed that the grounds maintenance will be sub-contracted with no storage requirement.
*   Broadband facility and IT points in the office and public rooms

## 7. Quality of Materials to be used
### 7.1 Building Exterior

7.2 A vandal-resistant design is required (with limited window and door openings), and a pitched roof with roof overhangs to deter access and provide protection to people and wall finishes. All to conform to the Design Code.

7.3 Roof lights with internal grilles are required in changing rooms and window and door openings may to be protected by roller shutters.

7.4 Wall finishes should deter graffiti and window frames should be of hardwood, aluminium or UPVC to reduce maintenance; external doors are to be robust with flush thresholds.

7.5 External Works

7.6 Disabled parking should be located near the main entrance and provision made for wheelchair parking. Car and cycle parking should meet overall SCDC standards. A separate service access should be provided.

7.7 Level paved access is required to the clubhouse, grass pitches and ATP's with good lighting to the building access and car park.

7.8 Boot scrapers are required outside the changing rooms and ATP

7.9 Building Interior

7.10 All materials are to be durable, especially doors which will experience high levels of use and potential misuse. A trophy cupboard should be provided, together with space for the hanging of team photographs.

7.11 Floors

7.12 Concrete with screed or power float finish, slip resistant ceramic tiles or sheet flooring to changing and shower areas. Heavy duty carpet in social areas

7.13 Walls

7.14 Impact resistant internal walls with ceramic tiles in changing and shower areas and solid core internal doors

7.15 Ceilings

7.16 Pitched roof areas with roof lights are to have a sloping ceiling to provide increased Headroom and spacious environment, moisture resistant tiles are to be used throughout changing, shower and circulation areas

7.17 Fittings

7.18 Robust slatted benches to be provided in changing rooms, mirrors and shelves in grooming areas and notice boards in general circulation spaces and entrance lobby.

7.19 Secure kit bag stores with slatted shelves and robust doors.

7.20 Coat hooks mounted over benches and in shower dry off areas, WCs. including public WCs.

7.21 Sanitary fittings

7.22 Stainless steel back to wall WC's and individual wall hung basins concealed pipe work to reduce vandalism and robust moisture resistant toilet cubicles complete with toilet roll holders and coat pegs.

7.23 Heating, Lighting and Ventilation

7.24 Good design is required to ensure a well-ventilated, comfortable environment, to minimise problems of condensation, mould, to minimise running costs and to reduce maintenance.

7.25 A sustainable design and energy solution is required for the sports hubs and proposals are to be fully evaluated and life cycle costs and benefits prepared.

7.26 The following suggestions are made given the expected usage patterns of the club
house but require further evaluation along with other low energy solutions before they are
incorporated into the design:

* Due to the expected high water demand from showering facilities, solar thermal
panels might provide a cost effective solution. The panels would be incorporated into the
roof structure to pre heat water for showers etc. but will require substantial water storage
provision.

* Under floor heating, coupled with the use of a Ground Source Heat pump should be
considered as a possibility given that the large sports field areas provide an ideal
opportunity for the installation of the underground pipes but the financial viability of the
technology must be evaluated. The heat pumps could provide a base load heating level
but would probably require a backup from gas fired boiler plant for extreme weather
conditions or in the event of failure of the heat pumps.

* Grey Water collection should be investigated as potentially advantageous, with water
collected predominantly from the shower fixtures and used for both WC flushing and
potentially as irrigation water for the pitches. Filtration plant will be required to ensure
the water is suitable for use.

* A Wind Turbine may be viable and should be investigated.

* Natural ventilation should be used wherever possible, but the high density occupation
of changing areas along with high humidity developed in showers will require mechanical
ventilation. This ventilation should be linked into the overall heating installation using a
heat recovery system.

* CCTV should be provided

**8. Management**
8.1 The ownership and management arrangements for the sports pavilion need to be
confirmed at an early stage as this would be a key influence on the design and specification
of the building but should provide a coordinated provision. The stakeholders will need to
consider ultimate responsibility for day-to-day operation and facility management. It is
envisaged that facilities management will be undertaken on a shared basis and may include
the following elements:
a) building and services maintenance
b) building cleaning services
c) caretaker and security services
d) environmental control
e) fire equipment
f) grounds maintenance services
g) utilities and statutory charges
h) waste storage/removal.
8.2 The details will be included in the overall Community Realm Management Strategy.

9. Trigger points

# APPENDIX 22 – PHASE 1 MANAGEMENT AND MAINTENANCE STRATEGY SUMMARY

## SUMMARY CONTENT OF MANAGEMENT AND MAINTENANCE STRATEGY

The section 106 makes provision for a Management and Maintenance Strategy to include agreement on, and not exclusively, the items set out in this Annex.

Reserved Matters and other planning applications made in respect of Phase 1 will be submitted to and approved by South Cambridgeshire District Council. This process will ensure the standard of the public realm reflects the high quality required at Northstowe. The approach to the management and maintenance of the public realm will be set out in the Strategy. This document will be approved by the Local Planning Authority and it will ensure the quality of the public realm is maintained whilst also providing good value for money.

A Steering Group will be set up to oversee the implementation of the Strategy.

**DEFINITION of public realm:**
a. Formal recreation/ sports pitches
b. Hard sports play areas including the synthetic turf pitch
c. Formal and informal MUGAs
d. Formal public space e.g. the landscaped square at the local centre, local park opposite the primary school
e. Informal open space, landscape and habitat
f. Equipped and non-equipped areas of play
g. Allotments/community orchard
h. Community building
i. Sports pavilion and associated parking
j. Structural planting
k. Strategic and non-strategic water bodies: Balancing lakes and ditches/swales
l. Existing trees and water bodies
m. Footpaths, bridleways and cycle ways

A full list of areas of public realm will be defined by reference to a plan and schedule in the Strategy.

**1. Indicative AREAS of public realm covered by the Management Strategy**

| Area of open space/water             | Size (ha) |
|--------------------------------------|-----------|
| Formal sports provision at Sports Hub | 6.17      |
| Public open space/ parks/ play space | 23.0      |
| Water bodies                         | 5.20      |
| Allotments/ community orchard                                | 1.57 |
| :----------------------------------------------------------- | :--- |
| Hatton's Road attenuation ponds (including water bodies)     | 25.00 |
| **Total open/space water**                                   | **60.94** |

II. **Confirmation of available FUNDING:**
*   Phase 1 Maintenance Contribution of £1,500,000 to be deployed in accordance with an agreed formula. Other sources of funding that could be used for management and maintenance of Phase 1 will be explored. These will include:
*   Hire charges from sports and community areas and to review differential hire charges in order to ensure that the hire charges are affordable for the local community and community groups
*   Annual Maintenance Charge to residents
*   Parish precept – the possibility of utilising this will be explored in conjunction with the local governance body at the time.
*   Any grants and donations

The possibility of using any potential retention of Business Rates from new businesses in Northstowe will be explored as the Strategy is developed.
The Management Strategy will identify the relevant funding sources in the development of the annual maintenance budgets for the management of the community realm.

III. FLEXIBILITY – The Management Strategy must be flexible and permit amendments to take account of changing governance and funding arrangements, subject to amongst other things the developer properly discharging relevant planning conditions and planning obligations.

IV. The Strategy should have a **COMPREHENSIVE** approach taking into account all the landscape, recreational, ecological and water/ drainage compartments and potentially be transferable across all future Phases of Northstowe. It will also have regard to Adoption issues.

V. Subject always to the provisions in Schedule 14 Part 4 the number of organisations involved in managing the community realm should be kept to a **MINIMUM**.

VI. The organisation(s) that manage the water bodies, water courses, access route and associated areas and structures that form part of the adoptable areas will need to comply with the requirements in Schedule 4 and actively participate in the Steering Group.
VII. The Strategy will explain the potential for the TRANSFER OF THE freehold of the COMMUNITY REALM to the District, Parish/Town Council. The transfer of the public realm in packages which enable discrete, efficient and practicable management to be undertaken will be important. In the interim period the phased construction and implementation of the Site will be undertaken in accordance with the submission made to discharge Planning Condition No.7. The Owners will put in place interim arrangements in the early years for the management and maintenance of the public realm which includes a liability period associated with the contract works undertaken by the Owners.

VIII. The Management arrangements in the early years when the Management Company is the nominated body must be COMMERCIALLY VIABLE, and thereafter they must be economically viable.

IX. The Management and Maintenance Strategy shall include relevant details in respect of frequency and performance targets for management and maintenance, an appropriate regime of hire charges of facilities hired out to the public and any relevant financial information which may be necessary so that management and maintenance responsibilities can be satisfactorily discharged.

X. The implementation of the Management and Maintenance Strategy and the Purposes for which the Phase 1 Maintenance Contribution together with any other potential funding sources will be used will be considered in the Terms of Reference for the Steering Group appended to the S106.

XI. A banking account is to be set up solely for the purpose of funding the management of the community realm. It will be the depository for the Northstowe Phase 1 Maintenance contribution and will be accessed in accordance with scheme that will be overseen by the steering group and will be set out as part of the management strategy. .

XII. The Terms of Reference for the Steering Group can include the establishment of a review mechanism subject to the material default provisions at paragraph 20 in the boilerplate clauses and the regimes for maintenance of the public realm agreed by the Local Planning Authority when discharging planning conditions or approving reserved matters submissions or other planning applications.
